================================================================================

FILE: src\features\lp_features.py

================================================================================

"""ATLAS-style LAG-SAFE label-propagation (LP) features for the seed transactions.

For every seed ``i`` scored at time ``t_i`` we count what the label system would ALREADY have told us
about the transactions linked to it through shared entities (card / device):

    lp_n_labeled   number of neighbours whose label is known at t_i
    lp_n_fraud     number of those that are confirmed fraud
    lp_fraud_rate  n_fraud / n_labeled   (0 when n_labeled = 0, never NaN)
    lp_has_fraud   1 if n_fraud > 0 else 0

Lag-safe vs the NAIVE LP feature
--------------------------------
The naive LP feature reads every neighbour label (``y_true``), including labels that arrive AFTER
``t_i``: it leaks the future. The lag-safe version only reads what is knowable at ``t_i``. This is the
feature-level arm of DelayGraphBench (ATLAS-style, per the project brief; the exact ATLAS definition
was NOT verified against its full text: KHÔNG CHẮC, CẦN VERIFY). Training still uses an ordinary loss.

Knowledge of neighbour ``j`` at the scoring time ``t_i`` (j must be STRICTLY earlier than i):

    P (confirmed fraud) : y_true_j == 1  AND  detection_time_j <= t_i
    N (matured negative): not P          AND  t_i - t_j >= W          (maturity window W, label-independent)
    U (unknown)         : everything else, never counted

This is exactly ``assign_pnu_masks`` evaluated with ``t_cut = t_i`` instead of ``t_train``.

How leakage is prevented
------------------------
1. ``y_true`` is read in ONE expression, ``(y == 1) & (detection_time <= t_seed)``. Wherever the gate is
   False the value of ``y_true`` has no effect: the labels of neighbours reported after ``t_i`` are
   invisible, whatever they are.
2. ``y_obs`` / ``pnu`` are NOT used: they are a snapshot at ``t_train``. For a train seed they would
   already contain labels that arrive between ``t_i`` and ``t_train`` (a leak); for a val/test seed they
   would hide labels that arrived between ``t_train`` and ``t_i``.
3. Negatives are matured by AGE (``t_i - t_j >= W``), not by the hidden delay of a legitimate row, so the
   N/U split never depends on the label.
4. The seed's own label is never read: only non-seed nodes are neighbours, they must be strictly earlier
   than the seed, and a node with the seed's own id is dropped (when ``n_id`` is available). Also, a seed
   always has ``detection_time > t_i``, so its own label can never pass the gate.
5. The transform must run BEFORE ``DropAttributes`` (it needs ``y_true``). ``build_neighbor_loader`` appends
   ``DropAttributes`` last; the transform raises if ``y_true`` is already gone.

Limits (read before interpreting results)
-----------------------------------------
* The counts cover the SAMPLED neighbours (<= ``num_neighbors[1]`` per entity under ``uniform``), not
  every neighbour in the graph: ``n_labeled`` saturates and the rate is a sample estimate.
* Only seeds get LP values. History nodes get zeros in the 4 columns (their own neighbourhood is not
  sampled), so the model sees 4 always-zero inputs on them.
* A neighbour reached through two entities is counted once (PyG disjoint sampling keeps one copy of a node
  per subgraph; KHÔNG CHẮC, CẦN VERIFY on a real batch).
* With uncapped delays a fraud that is already "mature" but still unreported counts as a negative
  (label noise, as in ``assign_pnu_masks``).
* ``negative_rule="detection_time"`` is the literal "detection_time <= t_i for both classes" reading: it
  uses the simulated delay of legitimate rows, which a real system does not have. Oracle-like ablation only.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Literal, Mapping

import torch
from torch import Tensor
from torch_geometric.data import HeteroData

from src.data.loader import SECONDS_PER_DAY
from src.graph.builder import TXN

LP_FEATURE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = ("lp_n_labeled", "lp_n_fraud", "lp_fraud_rate", "lp_has_fraud")
N_LP_FEATURES: int = len(LP_FEATURE_NAMES)
NegativeRule = Literal["maturity_window", "detection_time"]
NEGATIVE_RULES: tuple[str, ...] = ("maturity_window", "detection_time")
_REQUIRED_ATTRS: tuple[str, ...] = ("x", "time", "detection_time", "y_true", "batch", "batch_size")


def compute_lp_features(
    *,
    time: Tensor,
    detection_time: Tensor,
    y_true: Tensor,
    subgraph: Tensor,
    batch_size: int,
    maturity_window_days: float,
    negative_rule: NegativeRule = "maturity_window",
    node_id: Tensor | None = None,
) -> Tensor:
    """Raw LP features ``[batch_size, 4]`` (float32) for the first ``batch_size`` nodes (the seeds).

    Args:
        time: ``[n]`` int64 transaction time in seconds (seeds first, then history nodes).
        detection_time: ``[n]`` float seconds (``delay_simulator``); seed ``i`` has detection_time > time.
        y_true: ``[n]`` 0/1 final label. Read ONLY through the ``detection_time <= t_seed`` gate.
        subgraph: ``[n]`` subgraph id of each node (``batch['transaction'].batch``).
        batch_size: number of seeds = the first ``batch_size`` nodes.
        maturity_window_days: W, an unreported neighbour older than W counts as a negative.
        negative_rule: ``"maturity_window"`` (default) or ``"detection_time"`` (oracle-like ablation).
        node_id: optional ``[n]`` global ids; a history node with the seed's own id is ignored.
    """
    n = time.numel()
    bs = int(batch_size)
    if not (detection_time.numel() == n and y_true.numel() == n and subgraph.numel() == n):
        raise ValueError("time, detection_time, y_true and subgraph must have the same length.")
    if not 0 <= bs <= n:
        raise ValueError("batch_size must be between 0 and the number of transaction nodes.")
    if negative_rule not in NEGATIVE_RULES:
        raise ValueError(f"negative_rule must be one of {NEGATIVE_RULES}.")
    if maturity_window_days <= 0:
        raise ValueError("maturity_window_days must be > 0.")

    device = time.device
    out = torch.zeros(bs, N_LP_FEATURES, dtype=torch.float32, device=device)
    if bs == 0 or n == bs:  # no seeds or no history
        return out

    sub = subgraph.long()
    row_of_sub = torch.full((int(sub.max()) + 1,), -1, dtype=torch.long, device=device)
    row_of_sub[sub[:bs]] = torch.arange(bs, device=device)
    row = row_of_sub[sub[bs:]]  # owning seed row of every history node
    if bool((row < 0).any()):
        raise ValueError("A history node belongs to a subgraph without a seed.")

    t = time.to(torch.float64)
    det = detection_time.to(torch.float64)
    t_h, det_h, y_h = t[bs:], det[bs:], y_true[bs:]
    t_seed = t[:bs][row]

    usable = t_h < t_seed  # STRICTLY earlier than the seed (also removes the seed itself)
    if node_id is not None:
        usable = usable & (node_id[bs:] != node_id[:bs][row])

    # The only place where y_true is read: a label counts only if its report arrived by t_seed.
    reported = (y_h == 1) & (det_h <= t_seed)
    if negative_rule == "maturity_window":
        matured = (t_seed - t_h) >= float(maturity_window_days) * SECONDS_PER_DAY  # age only
    else:
        matured = det_h <= t_seed
    negative = (~reported) & matured

    labeled = usable & (reported | negative)
    fraud = usable & reported
    n_labeled = torch.bincount(row[labeled], minlength=bs).to(torch.float32)
    n_fraud = torch.bincount(row[fraud], minlength=bs).to(torch.float32)
    out[:, 0] = n_labeled
    out[:, 1] = n_fraud
    out[:, 2] = n_fraud / n_labeled.clamp(min=1.0)  # 0 when n_labeled == 0, never NaN
    out[:, 3] = (n_fraud > 0).to(torch.float32)
    return out


class LPFeatureTransform:
    """Batch transform: append the 4 LP columns to ``batch['transaction'].x`` (F -> F + 4).

    Must run BEFORE ``DropAttributes`` (it needs ``y_true``). Rows of history nodes get zeros; seed rows
    get the lag-safe features. ``log1p_counts`` applies ``log1p`` to the two count columns so they live on
    the scale of the z-scored features (the rate and the flag are already in [0, 1]).
    """

    n_features: int = N_LP_FEATURES
    names: tuple[str, ...] = LP_FEATURE_NAMES

    def __init__(
        self,
        maturity_window_days: float,
        negative_rule: NegativeRule = "maturity_window",
        log1p_counts: bool = True,
        node_type: str = TXN,
    ) -> None:
        if maturity_window_days <= 0:
            raise ValueError("maturity_window_days must be > 0.")
        if negative_rule not in NEGATIVE_RULES:
            raise ValueError(f"negative_rule must be one of {NEGATIVE_RULES}.")
        self.maturity_window_days = float(maturity_window_days)
        self.negative_rule = negative_rule
        self.log1p_counts = log1p_counts
        self.node_type = node_type

    def __call__(self, batch: HeteroData) -> HeteroData:
        store = batch[self.node_type]
        if "lp_dim" in store:
            raise RuntimeError("LP features were already appended to this batch.")
        missing = [k for k in _REQUIRED_ATTRS if k not in store]
        if missing:
            raise KeyError(
                f"LP transform needs {missing} on '{self.node_type}'. It must run BEFORE DropAttributes "
                "(y_true is removed afterwards) and on a disjoint temporal batch."
            )
        bs = int(store.batch_size)
        lp = compute_lp_features(
            time=store.time,
            detection_time=store.detection_time,
            y_true=store.y_true,
            subgraph=store.batch,
            batch_size=bs,
            maturity_window_days=self.maturity_window_days,
            negative_rule=self.negative_rule,
            node_id=store.get("n_id"),
        )
        if self.log1p_counts:
            lp[:, :2] = torch.log1p(lp[:, :2])
        full = store.x.new_zeros(store.x.size(0), N_LP_FEATURES)
        full[:bs] = lp.to(store.x.dtype)  # history rows stay zero
        store.x = torch.cat([store.x, full], dim=1)
        store.lp_dim = N_LP_FEATURES
        return batch


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class LPFeatureConfig:
    """The ``features.lp_features`` section of configs/base.yaml."""

    enabled: bool = False
    maturity_window_days: float | None = None  # None -> meta['config']['delay']['maturity_window_days']
    negative_rule: str = "maturity_window"
    log1p_counts: bool = True

    @classmethod
    def from_dict(cls, cfg: Mapping[str, Any] | None) -> "LPFeatureConfig":
        if not cfg:
            return cls()
        w = cfg.get("maturity_window_days")
        return cls(
            enabled=bool(cfg.get("enabled", False)),
            maturity_window_days=None if w is None else float(w),
            negative_rule=str(cfg.get("negative_rule", "maturity_window")),
            log1p_counts=bool(cfg.get("log1p_counts", True)),
        )


def lp_transform_from_config(
    features_cfg: Mapping[str, Any] | None, meta: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None
) -> LPFeatureTransform | None:
    """Return the transform, or ``None`` when ``features.lp_features.enabled`` is false (the default)."""
    cfg = LPFeatureConfig.from_dict((features_cfg or {}).get("lp_features"))
    if not cfg.enabled:
        return None
    window = cfg.maturity_window_days
    if window is None:
        window = None if meta is None else meta.get("config", {}).get("delay", {}).get("maturity_window_days")
    if window is None:
        raise ValueError("LP features need maturity_window_days (set it in the config or pass meta.json).")
    return LPFeatureTransform(float(window), cfg.negative_rule, cfg.log1p_counts)


__all__ = [
    "LP_FEATURE_NAMES", "N_LP_FEATURES", "compute_lp_features", "LPFeatureTransform",
    "LPFeatureConfig", "lp_transform_from_config",
]

================================================================================

FILE: src\graph\builder.py

================================================================================

"""Build PyG ``HeteroData`` phase graphs from the P/N/U-masked DataFrames.

Schema
------
Node types : ``transaction`` plus entity types ``card`` / ``device``
             (the email domain is a TRANSACTION FEATURE block, not a node type: see A5).
Edge types : ``(transaction, uses_<e>, <e>)`` and the reverse
             ``(<e>, rev_uses_<e>, transaction)``; both carry ``timestamp``
             (= ``TransactionDT`` of the transaction endpoint). The reverse
             edges are what lets a seed transaction reach its entities when
             PyG samples along incoming edges.

Phase graphs (cumulative, physically cut)
-----------------------------------------
``train`` = rows before ``t_train``; ``val`` = rows before ``t_val`` (train+val);
``test`` = every row. A graph never holds a node later than its own phase end,
so no Val/Test node can exist in the Train graph. Seeds of a phase graph are
the nodes of that phase (``seed_mask``); earlier nodes are history only.

Time semantics (node-level ``time``, needed by ``NeighborLoader(time_attr='time')``)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
* transaction ``time`` = ``TransactionDT`` (int64 seconds);
* entity ``time`` = FIRST-SEEN time (earliest linked transaction);
* the edge attribute is called ``timestamp``, never ``time``: PyG refuses a
  ``time_attr`` that exists on both nodes and edges.
Entity nodes carry NO statistic computed over the phase (no degree, no counts):
such a value would summarise transactions that are in a seed's future.

Assumptions (also in configs/base.yaml)
---------------------------------------
A1. No true card/device id exists in IEEE-CIS: entities are PROXY keys built from
    several columns, so graph quality depends on this construction.
A2. Super-nodes (entities with more than ``max_degree`` TRAIN transactions, e.g.
    a very common device string) are removed. The set is fitted on the train period only and
    reused for Val/Test, so no future information decides it. An entity that
    first appears after the train period is never removed (neighbour fan-out
    bounds the cost of sampling it).
A3. Entity ids are assigned in first-seen order over the whole timeline, so the
    id of an entity is identical in the train/val/test graphs and each phase
    graph holds exactly ids ``0..K_phase-1``. Ids are labels only.
A4. Numeric NaN are imputed with TRAIN medians and z-scored with TRAIN
    statistics; categories come from the train period (unseen -> ``__other__``).
A5. Numeric columns whose TRAIN missing ratio exceeds ``features.max_missing_ratio`` are dropped
    before any statistic is computed (validation/test never decide which columns survive).
    The email domain is encoded as transaction features (``EmailFeatureBlock``): train
    frequency + one-hot of the top-k TRAIN domains + other + missing. ``train_freq`` is a static
    statistic of the train period (no label, no val/test information), like the z-score mean/std.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from collections import Counter
import warnings
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Literal, Mapping

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import torch
from torch_geometric.data import HeteroData

from src.data.loader import (
    AMOUNT_COL,
    DETECTION_COL,
    ID_COL,
    IN_TRAIN_VIEW_COL,
    MISSING_TOKEN,
    NON_FEATURE_COLS,
    PNU_COL,
    TARGET_COL,
    TIME_COL,
    Y_OBS_COL,
    assert_no_label_columns,
)
from src.data.splitter import SplitBoundaries

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

TXN = "transaction"
Phase = Literal["train", "val", "test"]
SPLIT_CODES: dict[str, int] = {"train": 0, "val": 1, "test": 2}
PNU_CODES: dict[str, int] = {"P": 0, "N": 1, "U": 2}
MISSING_RATIO_NAME = "__missing_ratio"


class GraphIntegrityError(ValueError):
    """A phase graph violates a causality / masking invariant."""


def _tensor(a: np.ndarray) -> torch.Tensor:
    """numpy -> torch with an owned, writable copy (pandas 3 hands out read-only views)."""
    return torch.from_numpy(np.array(a, copy=True))


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Configuration
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EntitySpec:
    """One entity type. ``key_cols`` form the (proxy) key; a row gets an edge only
    if every column in ``required_cols`` is non-null. ``max_degree=None`` keeps all."""

    name: str
    key_cols: tuple[str, ...]
    required_cols: tuple[str, ...]
    max_degree: int | None = 500


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EmailSpec:
    """``graph.features.email``: email domain -> transaction features.

    An empty ``cols`` disables the block.
    """

    cols: tuple[str, ...] = ("P_emaildomain",)
    top_k: int = 10
    add_frequency: bool = True

    @classmethod
    def from_dict(cls, d: Mapping[str, Any] | None) -> "EmailSpec":
        d = dict(d or {})
        cols = d.get("cols", cls.cols)
        cols = (cols,) if isinstance(cols, str) else tuple(cols)
        return cls(
            cols=cols,
            top_k=int(d.get("top_k", cls.top_k)),
            add_frequency=bool(d.get("add_frequency", cls.add_frequency)),
        )


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FeatureSpec:
    onehot_cols: tuple[str, ...] = (
        "ProductCD", "card4", "card6", "M1", "M2", "M3", "M4", "M5", "M6", "M7", "M8", "M9",
    )
    numeric_exclude: tuple[str, ...] = ("card1", "addr1", "addr2")
    max_categories: int = 30
    clip_z: float | None = 10.0
    add_missing_ratio: bool = True
    min_std: float = 1e-6
    max_missing_ratio: float | None = 0.9   # drop numeric cols with TRAIN missing ratio > this; None = off
    email: EmailSpec = field(default_factory=EmailSpec)


DEFAULT_ENTITIES: tuple[EntitySpec, ...] = (
    EntitySpec("card", ("card1", "card2", "card3", "card4", "card5", "card6"), ("card1",), 500),
    EntitySpec("device", ("DeviceInfo", "id_30", "id_31"), ("DeviceInfo",), 500),
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GraphConfig:
    entities: tuple[EntitySpec, ...] = DEFAULT_ENTITIES
    features: FeatureSpec = field(default_factory=FeatureSpec)

    @classmethod
    def from_dict(cls, cfg: Mapping[str, Any] | None) -> "GraphConfig":
        """Build from the ``graph:`` section of configs/base.yaml."""
        if not cfg:
            return cls()
        ents = tuple(
            EntitySpec(
                name=name,
                key_cols=tuple(e["key_cols"]),
                required_cols=tuple(e.get("required_cols", e["key_cols"][:1])),
                max_degree=e.get("max_degree", 500),
            )
            for name, e in cfg.get("entities", {}).items()
        ) or DEFAULT_ENTITIES
        f = dict(cfg.get("features", {}))
        for k in ("onehot_cols", "numeric_exclude"):
            if k in f:
                f[k] = tuple(f[k])
        f["email"] = EmailSpec.from_dict(f.get("email"))  # dict | None -> EmailSpec
        return cls(entities=ents, features=FeatureSpec(**f))


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Train-only column filter + email feature block
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def select_columns_by_missing_ratio(
    train_df: pd.DataFrame,
    columns: list[str],
    max_missing_ratio: float | None,
) -> tuple[list[str], dict[str, float]]:
    """Drop columns whose missing ratio on the TRAIN frame is > ``max_missing_ratio``.

    Only ``train_df`` is inspected. ``None`` disables the filter; 1.0 keeps everything.
    Column-wise loop on purpose: ``train_df[cols].isna()`` would allocate a full boolean frame.

    Returns ``(kept columns in original order, {dropped column: train missing ratio})``.
    """
    cols = list(columns)
    if max_missing_ratio is None or not cols:
        return cols, {}
    if not 0.0 < float(max_missing_ratio) <= 1.0:
        raise ValueError("max_missing_ratio must be in (0, 1] or None.")
    n = len(train_df)
    if n == 0:
        raise ValueError("Empty train frame: cannot compute missing ratios.")
    dropped: dict[str, float] = {}
    for c in cols:
        r = float(train_df[c].isna().sum()) / n
        if r > max_missing_ratio:
            dropped[c] = r
    kept = [c for c in cols if c not in dropped]
    if not kept:
        raise ValueError("max_missing_ratio removed every numeric column.")
    return kept, dropped


class EmailFeatureBlock:
    """Email domain -> numeric features of the transaction node (no email node / edges).

    Per column in ``spec.cols``: ``[train_freq] + one-hot(top_k TRAIN domains) + is_other + is_missing``.
    Top-k list and frequencies come from the frame given to :meth:`fit` (TRAIN) only; a domain
    unseen in train gets ``train_freq = 0`` and ``is_other = 1``. Values are lower-cased and
    stripped; NaN maps to ``MISSING_TOKEN`` (the sentinel is never a "top domain").
    """

    def __init__(self, spec: EmailSpec) -> None:
        if spec.cols and spec.top_k < 1:
            raise ValueError("email.top_k must be >= 1.")
        self.spec = spec
        self.top_domains: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
        self.train_freq: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}

    @staticmethod
    def _values(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> np.ndarray:
        """Cleaned domains as an object array (a missing column counts as all-missing)."""
        if col not in df.columns:
            return np.full(len(df), MISSING_TOKEN, dtype=object)
        s = df[col].astype("string").fillna(MISSING_TOKEN).str.strip().str.lower()
        return s.to_numpy(dtype=object)

    def fit(self, train_df: pd.DataFrame) -> "EmailFeatureBlock":
        if len(train_df) == 0:
            raise ValueError("Empty train frame: cannot fit email features.")
        for col in self.spec.cols:
            if col not in train_df.columns:
                raise KeyError(f"Email column '{col}' not found in the train frame.")
            vals = self._values(train_df, col)
            counts = Counter(vals.tolist())
            n = float(len(vals))
            self.train_freq[col] = {str(d): c / n for d, c in counts.items()}
            ranked = sorted(((c, str(d)) for d, c in counts.items() if d != MISSING_TOKEN),
                            key=lambda t: (-t[0], t[1]))  # count desc, name asc: deterministic
            self.top_domains[col] = [d for _, d in ranked[: self.spec.top_k]]
        return self

    @property
    def feature_names(self) -> list[str]:
        names: list[str] = []
        for col in self.spec.cols:
            if self.spec.add_frequency:
                names.append(f"email:{col}:train_freq")
            names += [f"email:{col}:is:{d}" for d in self.top_domains[col]]
            names += [f"email:{col}:is_other", f"email:{col}:is_missing"]
        return names

    def transform(self, df: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:
        """float32 ``[len(df), len(feature_names)]``, no NaN."""
        if not self.top_domains:
            raise RuntimeError("EmailFeatureBlock.fit must be called first.")
        blocks: list[np.ndarray] = []
        for col in self.spec.cols:
            vals = self._values(df, col)
            n = len(vals)
            if self.spec.add_frequency:
                fmap = self.train_freq[col]
                freq = np.fromiter((fmap.get(v, 0.0) for v in vals), dtype=np.float32, count=n)
                blocks.append(freq.reshape(-1, 1))
            top = self.top_domains[col]
            onehot = (np.stack([vals == d for d in top], axis=1).astype(np.float32)
                      if top else np.zeros((n, 0), dtype=np.float32))
            is_missing = vals == MISSING_TOKEN
            is_other = ~((onehot.sum(axis=1) > 0) | is_missing)
            blocks += [onehot,
                       is_other.reshape(-1, 1).astype(np.float32),
                       is_missing.reshape(-1, 1).astype(np.float32)]
        return np.concatenate(blocks, axis=1)

    def state_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {"spec": asdict(self.spec), "top_domains": self.top_domains, "train_freq": self.train_freq}


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Transaction features (fitted on TRAIN only)
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
class TxnFeatureEncoder:
    """Median-impute + z-score numeric columns, one-hot a few categoricals.

    Every statistic comes from the frame given to :meth:`fit` (the train period).
    ``TransactionAmt`` is log1p-transformed first. Constant columns are dropped.
    """

    def __init__(self, spec: FeatureSpec | None = None) -> None:
        self.spec = spec or FeatureSpec()
        self.numeric_cols: list[str] = []
        self.median = np.empty(0, dtype=np.float32)
        self.mean = np.empty(0, dtype=np.float32)
        self.std = np.empty(0, dtype=np.float32)
        self.onehot: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
        self.fitted = False
        self.dropped_missing_cols: dict[str, float] = {}  # {col: TRAIN missing ratio}, filled by fit
        self.email_block: EmailFeatureBlock | None = None

    # -- helpers ----------------------------------------------------------- #
    def _numeric_matrix(self, df: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:
        missing = [c for c in self.numeric_cols if c not in df.columns]
        if missing:
            logger.warning("%d numeric feature column(s) absent (imputed): %s...", len(missing), missing[:5])
        X = df.reindex(columns=self.numeric_cols).to_numpy(dtype=np.float32, copy=True)
        if AMOUNT_COL in self.numeric_cols:
            j = self.numeric_cols.index(AMOUNT_COL)
            X[:, j] = np.log1p(np.maximum(X[:, j], 0.0))
        return X

    # -- fit / transform --------------------------------------------------- #
    def fit(self, train_df: pd.DataFrame) -> "TxnFeatureEncoder":
        sp = self.spec
        banned = set(NON_FEATURE_COLS) | set(sp.numeric_exclude) | set(sp.onehot_cols)
        cand = [
            c for c in train_df.columns
            if c not in banned
            and pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(train_df[c])
            and not pd.api.types.is_bool_dtype(train_df[c])
        ]
        assert_no_label_columns(cand + [c for c in sp.onehot_cols if c in train_df.columns])
        # Drop mostly-missing numeric columns using TRAIN only, BEFORE any statistic is computed.
        cand, self.dropped_missing_cols = select_columns_by_missing_ratio(
            train_df, cand, sp.max_missing_ratio
        )
        self.numeric_cols = cand
        X = self._numeric_matrix(train_df)
        with warnings.catch_warnings():
            warnings.simplefilter("ignore", category=RuntimeWarning)  # all-NaN columns
            med = np.nanmedian(X, axis=0)
        ok = np.isfinite(med)
        X = np.where(np.isnan(X), med, X)
        mean = X.mean(axis=0, dtype=np.float64)
        std = X.std(axis=0, dtype=np.float64)
        ok &= std > sp.min_std
        dropped = int((~ok).sum())
        if dropped:
            logger.info("Dropping %d constant / all-missing numeric columns (train).", dropped)
        keep = np.flatnonzero(ok)
        self.numeric_cols = [self.numeric_cols[i] for i in keep]
        self.median = med[keep].astype(np.float32)
        self.mean = mean[keep].astype(np.float32)
        self.std = std[keep].astype(np.float32)

        self.onehot = {}
        for c in sp.onehot_cols:
            if c not in train_df.columns:
                continue
            counts = train_df[c].astype("string").value_counts()
            self.onehot[c] = [str(v) for v in counts.index[: sp.max_categories]]
        self.email_block = EmailFeatureBlock(sp.email).fit(train_df) if sp.email.cols else None
        self.fitted = True
        return self

    def transform(self, df: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:
        if not self.fitted:
            raise RuntimeError("TxnFeatureEncoder.fit must be called first.")
        X = self._numeric_matrix(df)
        miss = np.isnan(X)
        ratio = miss.mean(axis=1, dtype=np.float32) if X.shape[1] else np.zeros(len(df), np.float32)
        X = np.where(miss, self.median, X)
        X = (X - self.mean) / self.std
        if self.spec.clip_z is not None:
            np.clip(X, -self.spec.clip_z, self.spec.clip_z, out=X)
        parts: list[np.ndarray] = [X.astype(np.float32, copy=False)]
        for c, cats in self.onehot.items():
            s = df[c].astype("string") if c in df.columns else pd.Series(pd.NA, index=df.index, dtype="string")
            isna = s.isna().to_numpy()
            cols = [(s == cat).to_numpy(dtype=bool, na_value=False) for cat in cats]
            known = np.logical_or.reduce(cols) if cols else np.zeros(len(df), dtype=bool)
            cols += [isna, ~isna & ~known]  # <c>=__nan__, <c>=__other__
            parts.append(np.stack(cols, axis=1).astype(np.float32))
        if self.email_block is not None:  # NEW: must sit at the same position as in feature_names
            parts.append(self.email_block.transform(df))
        if self.spec.add_missing_ratio:
            parts.append(ratio.reshape(-1, 1))
        return np.concatenate(parts, axis=1)

    @property
    def feature_names(self) -> list[str]:
        names = list(self.numeric_cols)
        for c, cats in self.onehot.items():
            names += [f"{c}={v}" for v in cats] + [f"{c}=__nan__", f"{c}=__other__"]
        if self.email_block is not None:  # same order as transform
            names += self.email_block.feature_names
        if self.spec.add_missing_ratio:
            names.append(MISSING_RATIO_NAME)
        return names

    def state_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "spec": asdict(self.spec),
            "numeric_cols": list(self.numeric_cols),
            "median": self.median.tolist(),
            "mean": self.mean.tolist(),
            "std": self.std.tolist(),
            "onehot": {k: list(v) for k, v in self.onehot.items()},
            "dropped_missing_cols": dict(self.dropped_missing_cols),
            "email": self.email_block.state_dict() if self.email_block is not None else None,
        }


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Entity keys
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def make_entity_keys(df: pd.DataFrame, spec: EntitySpec) -> pd.Series:
    """String key per row (``<NA>`` where the row has no valid entity).

    NaN inside a *non-required* key column becomes the token ``NA`` so that the
    key stays comparable across phases.
    """
    cols = [c for c in spec.key_cols if c in df.columns]
    req = [c for c in spec.required_cols if c in df.columns]
    if not cols or len(req) < len(spec.required_cols):
        return pd.Series(pd.NA, index=df.index, dtype="string")
    valid = df[req].notna().all(axis=1)
    parts = df[cols].astype("string").fillna("NA")
    keys = parts.iloc[:, 0]
    for c in parts.columns[1:]:
        keys = keys + "|" + parts[c]
    return keys.where(valid)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Builder
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
class GraphBuilder:
    """Fit once on the train period, then ``build`` one graph per phase."""

    def __init__(self, config: GraphConfig | None = None) -> None:
        self.config = config or GraphConfig()
        self.encoder = TxnFeatureEncoder(self.config.features)
        self.vocab: dict[str, pd.Index] = {}
        self.entity_stats: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
        self.boundaries: SplitBoundaries | None = None
        self.fitted = False

    def fit(
        self,
        train_df: pd.DataFrame,
        val_df: pd.DataFrame,
        test_df: pd.DataFrame,
        boundaries: SplitBoundaries,
    ) -> "GraphBuilder":
        """Fit encoder + super-node set on TRAIN; build entity id vocabularies.

        ``val_df``/``test_df`` are used ONLY to assign stable entity ids (first-seen
        order); no statistic of theirs reaches the features or the super-node set.
        """
        self.encoder.fit(train_df)
        self.boundaries = boundaries
        key_cols = sorted({c for s in self.config.entities for c in (*s.key_cols, *s.required_cols)})
        keep_cols = [c for c in key_cols if c in train_df.columns]
        full = pd.concat([f[keep_cols] for f in (train_df, val_df, test_df)], ignore_index=True)
        n_train = len(train_df)

        for spec in self.config.entities:
            keys = make_entity_keys(full, spec)
            tr_keys = keys.iloc[:n_train].dropna()
            deg = tr_keys.value_counts()
            capped = spec.max_degree is not None
            super_keys = deg.index[deg > spec.max_degree] if capped else deg.index[:0]
            kept = keys[~keys.isin(super_keys)].dropna()
            self.vocab[spec.name] = pd.Index(pd.unique(kept))  # first-seen order (time-sorted input)
            n_edges = int(len(tr_keys))
            dropped = int(deg[deg.index.isin(super_keys)].sum()) if len(super_keys) else 0
            q = deg.quantile([0.5, 0.9, 0.99]) if len(deg) else pd.Series([0, 0, 0], index=[0.5, 0.9, 0.99])
            self.entity_stats[spec.name] = {
                "n_entities_train": int(len(deg)),
                "n_super_removed": int(len(super_keys)),
                "train_edges": n_edges,
                "train_edges_dropped": dropped,
                "train_edges_dropped_%": 100.0 * dropped / max(n_edges, 1),
                "deg_p50": float(q.iloc[0]), "deg_p90": float(q.iloc[1]), "deg_p99": float(q.iloc[2]),
                "deg_max": float(deg.max()) if len(deg) else 0.0,
                "max_degree_cap": float(spec.max_degree) if capped else float("inf"),
                "vocab_size_all_phases": int(len(self.vocab[spec.name])),
            }
        self.fitted = True
        return self

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    def build(self, df: pd.DataFrame, phase: Phase) -> HeteroData:
        """Build the graph of ``phase`` from the cumulative, time-sorted frame ``df``.

        ``df`` must be train (phase=train), train+val (val) or train+val+test (test),
        already passed through ``simulate_delay`` and ``assign_pnu_masks``.
        """
        if not self.fitted or self.boundaries is None:
            raise RuntimeError("GraphBuilder.fit must be called first.")
        b = self.boundaries
        t = df[TIME_COL].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64)
        if len(t) > 1 and (np.diff(t) < 0).any():
            raise GraphIntegrityError("Input frame must be sorted by TransactionDT.")
        t_end = {"train": b.t_train, "val": b.t_val, "test": b.t_end}[phase]
        if len(t) == 0 or (t >= t_end).any():
            raise GraphIntegrityError(f"Rows at/after the end of the '{phase}' phase (t_end={t_end}).")

        split = ((t >= b.t_train).astype(np.int8) + (t >= b.t_val).astype(np.int8)).astype(np.int8)
        data = HeteroData()
        txn = data[TXN]
        x = self.encoder.transform(df)
        txn.x = torch.from_numpy(x)
        txn.time = _tensor(t)
        txn.detection_time = _tensor(df[DETECTION_COL].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64))
        txn.amount = _tensor(df[AMOUNT_COL].to_numpy(dtype=np.float32))
        txn.y_obs = _tensor(df[Y_OBS_COL].to_numpy(dtype=np.int8))
        txn.pnu = _tensor(df[PNU_COL].astype(str).map(PNU_CODES).to_numpy(dtype=np.int8))
        txn.in_train_view = _tensor(df[IN_TRAIN_VIEW_COL].to_numpy(dtype=bool))
        # Evaluation / oracle ONLY. The dataset layer strips it unless explicitly allowed.
        txn.y_true = _tensor(df[TARGET_COL].to_numpy(dtype=np.int8))
        txn.split = _tensor(split)
        txn.tid = _tensor(df[ID_COL].to_numpy(dtype=np.int64))
        txn.seed_mask = _tensor(split == SPLIT_CODES[phase])

        for spec in self.config.entities:
            keys = make_entity_keys(df, spec)
            valid = keys.notna().to_numpy()
            codes = np.full(len(df), -1, dtype=np.int64)
            if valid.any():
                codes[valid] = self.vocab[spec.name].get_indexer(keys[valid])
            src = np.flatnonzero(codes >= 0)
            if src.size == 0:
                logger.warning("Entity '%s' has no edges in the %s graph; type dropped.", spec.name, phase)
                continue
            dst = codes[src]
            n_ent = int(dst.max()) + 1
            uniq, first_pos = np.unique(dst, return_index=True)  # df is time-sorted: first = earliest
            if uniq.size != n_ent:  # ids must be contiguous (assumption A3)
                raise GraphIntegrityError(f"Entity ids of '{spec.name}' are not contiguous in the {phase} graph.")
            ts = t[src]
            ent = data[spec.name]
            ent.node_id = torch.arange(n_ent, dtype=torch.long)
            ent.time = _tensor(ts[first_pos].astype(np.int64))  # first-seen time
            ent.num_nodes = n_ent
            fwd = _tensor(np.stack([src, dst]))
            data[(TXN, f"uses_{spec.name}", spec.name)].edge_index = fwd
            data[(TXN, f"uses_{spec.name}", spec.name)].timestamp = _tensor(ts)
            data[(spec.name, f"rev_uses_{spec.name}", TXN)].edge_index = fwd.flip(0).contiguous()
            data[(spec.name, f"rev_uses_{spec.name}", TXN)].timestamp = _tensor(ts)

        data.phase = phase
        data.t_end = float(t_end)
        data.t_train = float(b.t_train)
        data.t_val = float(b.t_val)
        data.label_cut = float(b.t_train)  # y_obs / pnu are defined at this instant
        data.feature_names = self.encoder.feature_names
        return data

    def state_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "config": {"entities": [asdict(e) for e in self.config.entities], "features": asdict(self.config.features)},
            "encoder": self.encoder.state_dict(),
            "vocab_sizes": {k: int(len(v)) for k, v in self.vocab.items()},
            "entity_stats": self.entity_stats,
            "boundaries": asdict(self.boundaries) if self.boundaries else None,
        }


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Static integrity checks (used by prepare_data and tests/test_leakage.py)
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def validate_phase_graph(data: HeteroData) -> None:
    """Raise ``GraphIntegrityError`` listing every violated invariant.

    Checks: no node/edge later than the phase end; no Val/Test node in an earlier
    graph; seeds = the phase's own nodes; edge ``timestamp`` = time of its
    transaction endpoint; forward/reverse edge consistency; entity ``time`` =
    first-seen time; ``time`` only on nodes; label masking at the label cut;
    no label-derived feature name.
    """
    errs: list[str] = []
    phase = data.phase
    code = SPLIT_CODES[phase]
    txn = data[TXN]
    t = txn.time.numpy()

    if (t >= data.t_end).any():
        errs.append("transaction node at/after the end of the phase (future node in graph)")
    if len(t) > 1 and (np.diff(t) < 0).any():
        errs.append("transaction nodes are not sorted by time")
    split = txn.split.numpy()
    if (split > code).any():
        errs.append("node from a LATER phase present (e.g. Val/Test node in the Train graph)")
    expected = np.where(t >= data.t_val, 2, np.where(t >= data.t_train, 1, 0))
    if not np.array_equal(split, expected):
        errs.append("split codes disagree with the time boundaries")
    if not np.array_equal(txn.seed_mask.numpy(), split == code):
        errs.append("seed_mask differs from the phase's own nodes")

    for store in data.edge_stores:
        if "time" in store:
            errs.append("an edge store has a 'time' attribute (PyG forbids node+edge time)")
    for nt in data.node_types:
        if "time" not in data[nt]:
            errs.append(f"node type '{nt}' has no 'time'")

    for ent in [n for n in data.node_types if n != TXN]:
        fkey, rkey = (TXN, f"uses_{ent}", ent), (ent, f"rev_uses_{ent}", TXN)
        if fkey not in data.edge_types or rkey not in data.edge_types:
            errs.append(f"entity '{ent}': forward or reverse edge type missing")
            continue
        fe, re = data[fkey], data[rkey]
        src, dst = fe.edge_index[0].numpy(), fe.edge_index[1].numpy()
        n_ent = data[ent].num_nodes
        if src.size and (src.max() >= len(t) or dst.max() >= n_ent or src.min() < 0 or dst.min() < 0):
            errs.append(f"entity '{ent}': edge index out of range")
            continue
        if not torch.equal(re.edge_index, fe.edge_index.flip(0)):
            errs.append(f"entity '{ent}': reverse edges do not mirror forward edges")
        if not torch.equal(re.timestamp, fe.timestamp):
            errs.append(f"entity '{ent}': reverse timestamps differ")
        ts = fe.timestamp.numpy()
        if not np.array_equal(ts, t[src]):
            errs.append(f"entity '{ent}': edge timestamp != TransactionDT of its transaction")
        first = np.full(n_ent, np.iinfo(np.int64).max, dtype=np.int64)
        np.minimum.at(first, dst, ts)
        if not np.array_equal(first, data[ent].time.numpy()):
            errs.append(f"entity '{ent}': node time is not the first-seen time")

    y_obs, det = txn.y_obs.numpy(), txn.detection_time.numpy()
    late = det > data.label_cut
    if (y_obs[late] != 0).any():
        errs.append("y_obs == 1 for a transaction detected after the label cut")
    if (y_obs[~txn.in_train_view.numpy()] != 0).any():
        errs.append("y_obs == 1 outside the train view")
    if not np.array_equal(txn.pnu.numpy() == PNU_CODES["P"], y_obs == 1):
        errs.append("pnu==P is not equivalent to y_obs==1")
    if (txn.y_true.numpy()[y_obs == 1] != 1).any():
        errs.append("observed positive that is not a true fraud")
    try:
        assert_no_label_columns(data.feature_names)
    except ValueError as exc:
        errs.append(str(exc))

    if errs:
        raise GraphIntegrityError(f"{phase} graph violates {len(errs)} invariant(s): " + "; ".join(errs))


__all__ = [
    "TXN", "SPLIT_CODES", "PNU_CODES", "GraphIntegrityError", "EntitySpec", "EmailSpec",
    "FeatureSpec", "GraphConfig", "TxnFeatureEncoder", "make_entity_keys", "GraphBuilder",
    "validate_phase_graph", "EmailFeatureBlock", "select_columns_by_missing_ratio",
]

================================================================================

FILE: src\graph\sampler.py

================================================================================

"""Causal ``NeighborLoader`` for the phase graphs (no peeking at the future).

How causality is enforced
-------------------------
* Every node type has an int64 ``time`` (transaction time / entity first-seen
  time) and edges carry ``timestamp`` instead. ``time_attr='time'`` therefore
  selects *node-level* temporal sampling, where a sampled neighbour must have
  ``time <= seed_time``.
* ``strict_time=True`` (default) feeds ``input_time = time - 1`` for every
  seed. With integer seconds this makes neighbours STRICTLY earlier than the
  seed, so same-second transactions are never visible (PyG alone would allow
  equality). Consequence: an entity whose first-seen time equals the seed's own
  time (a brand-new card/device) is not sampled; the seed simply has no history
  through it, which is the causal truth.
* Temporal sampling is always *disjoint*: each seed owns its subgraph (more
  memory than the shared-subgraph mode) and the batch holds ``batch[node_type].batch``.

Backend requirement
-------------------
Temporal sampling needs ``pyg-lib``. With ``torch-sparse`` only, PyG refuses
disjoint sampling. We raise instead of silently falling back to a
non-temporal sampler, which would leak the future.

``y_true`` is stripped from every mini-batch unless ``expose_true_labels=True``.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Callable, Mapping, Sequence

import torch
from torch import Tensor
from torch_geometric.data import HeteroData
from torch_geometric.loader import NeighborLoader

from src.graph.builder import TXN

TIME_ATTR = "time"
TEMPORAL_STRATEGIES = ("uniform", "last")


class CausalityError(AssertionError):
    """A sampled mini-batch contains a node or edge from the seed's future."""


def has_pyg_lib() -> bool:
    import torch_geometric.typing as pyg_typing

    return bool(getattr(pyg_typing, "WITH_PYG_LIB", False))


def require_pyg_lib() -> None:
    if not has_pyg_lib():
        raise ImportError(
            "Temporal NeighborLoader needs 'pyg-lib' (torch-sparse cannot do disjoint/temporal "
            "sampling). Install the wheel matching your torch/CUDA build, e.g. "
            "`pip install pyg_lib -f https://data.pyg.org/whl/torch-<TORCH>+<CUDA>.html`. "
            "Refusing to fall back to a non-temporal sampler (it would leak the future)."
        )


class DropAttributes:
    """Batch transform removing attributes (default: ``y_true``) from the seed type."""

    def __init__(self, names: Sequence[str], node_type: str = TXN) -> None:
        self.names = tuple(names)
        self.node_type = node_type

    def __call__(self, batch: HeteroData) -> HeteroData:
        store = batch[self.node_type]
        for name in self.names:
            if name in store:
                del store[name]
        return batch


class ComposeTransforms:
    """Apply batch transforms in order (LP features first, ``DropAttributes`` always last)."""

    def __init__(self, steps: Sequence[Callable[[HeteroData], HeteroData]]) -> None:
        self.steps = list(steps)

    def __call__(self, batch: HeteroData) -> HeteroData:
        for step in self.steps:
            batch = step(batch)
        return batch


def _validate_graph_for_sampling(data: HeteroData, input_type: str) -> None:
    if input_type not in data.node_types:
        raise KeyError(f"Seed node type '{input_type}' not in graph.")
    for nt in data.node_types:
        t = data[nt].get(TIME_ATTR)
        if t is None:
            raise ValueError(f"Node type '{nt}' has no '{TIME_ATTR}'; temporal sampling needs it on every type.")
        if t.dtype != torch.long:
            raise ValueError(f"'{nt}.{TIME_ATTR}' must be int64 (got {t.dtype}); strict_time relies on integers.")
    if any(TIME_ATTR in s for s in data.edge_stores):
        raise ValueError(f"Edges must not carry '{TIME_ATTR}' (use 'timestamp'): PyG forbids node+edge time.")


def make_seed_time(data: HeteroData, seed_idx: Tensor, strict_time: bool, input_type: str = TXN) -> Tensor:
    """Time handed to the sampler for each seed (``time - 1`` when strict)."""
    t = data[input_type][TIME_ATTR][seed_idx]
    return t - 1 if strict_time else t.clone()


def build_neighbor_loader(
    data: HeteroData,
    num_neighbors: Sequence[int] | Mapping[object, Sequence[int]],
    batch_size: int,
    *,
    seed_mask: Tensor | None = None,
    shuffle: bool = False,
    strict_time: bool = True,
    temporal_strategy: str = "uniform",
    num_workers: int = 0,
    drop_last: bool = False,
    expose_true_labels: bool = False,
    input_type: str = TXN,
    batch_transforms: Sequence[Callable[[HeteroData], HeteroData]] = (),
    check_backend: bool = True,
    **loader_kwargs,
) -> NeighborLoader:
    """Create a temporal, disjoint ``NeighborLoader`` over the seeds of ``data``.

    Args:
        data: a phase graph from ``GraphBuilder.build`` (node ``time``, edge ``timestamp``).
        num_neighbors: fan-out per hop, e.g. ``[15, 10]`` (applied to every edge type), or a
            dict keyed by edge type. A transaction has at most one neighbour per entity type in
            hop 1, so the first number is rarely binding.
        batch_size: number of seed transactions per mini-batch.
        seed_mask: bool mask over ``input_type`` nodes; defaults to ``data[input_type].seed_mask``.
        strict_time: neighbours strictly earlier than the seed (``input_time = time - 1``).
        temporal_strategy: ``uniform`` or ``last`` (most recent neighbours).
        expose_true_labels: keep ``y_true`` in batches (evaluation / oracle runs only).
        batch_transforms: callables run on every batch BEFORE ``DropAttributes`` (e.g. LPFeatureTransform,
            which needs ``y_true``). ``DropAttributes`` is always appended last unless
            ``expose_true_labels=True``.
        check_backend: raise if ``pyg-lib`` is missing. Only set False to build the object
            for inspection; iterating it without the backend fails anyway.
    """
    if temporal_strategy not in TEMPORAL_STRATEGIES:
        raise ValueError(f"temporal_strategy must be one of {TEMPORAL_STRATEGIES}.")
    if batch_size <= 0:
        raise ValueError("batch_size must be > 0.")
    if isinstance(num_neighbors, Sequence) and (len(num_neighbors) == 0 or any(int(k) <= 0 for k in num_neighbors)):
        raise ValueError("num_neighbors must be a non-empty list of positive ints (e.g. [15, 10]).")
    if check_backend:
        require_pyg_lib()
    _validate_graph_for_sampling(data, input_type)

    if seed_mask is None:
        seed_mask = data[input_type].get("seed_mask")
        if seed_mask is None:
            raise ValueError("No seed_mask given and the graph has none.")
    seed_idx = torch.nonzero(seed_mask, as_tuple=False).view(-1)
    if seed_idx.numel() == 0:
        raise ValueError("No seed nodes selected.")
    input_time = make_seed_time(data, seed_idx, strict_time, input_type)

    steps = list(batch_transforms)
    if not expose_true_labels:
        steps.append(DropAttributes(("y_true",), node_type=input_type))  # ALWAYS last
    transform = None if not steps else (steps[0] if len(steps) == 1 else ComposeTransforms(steps))

    return NeighborLoader(
        data,
        num_neighbors=list(num_neighbors) if not isinstance(num_neighbors, Mapping) else dict(num_neighbors),
        input_nodes=(input_type, seed_idx),
        input_time=input_time,
        time_attr=TIME_ATTR,
        temporal_strategy=temporal_strategy,
        batch_size=batch_size,
        shuffle=shuffle,
        drop_last=drop_last,
        num_workers=num_workers,
        transform=transform,
        **loader_kwargs,
    )


def verify_batch_causality(batch: HeteroData, *, strict_time: bool = True, input_type: str = TXN) -> None:
    """Assert that no sampled node/edge is later than its seed (use in tests / debugging).

    Relies on the disjoint layout: ``batch[nt].batch`` maps each node to its seed's
    subgraph and ``batch[input_type].seed_time`` holds the time given to the sampler.
    The first ``batch_size`` nodes of ``input_type`` are the seeds themselves and are skipped.
    """
    store = batch[input_type]
    bs = int(store.batch_size)
    seed_time = store.seed_time
    if seed_time.numel() != bs:
        raise CausalityError("seed_time and batch_size disagree.")

    for nt in batch.node_types:
        ns = batch[nt]
        if "batch" not in ns:
            raise CausalityError(f"'{nt}' has no subgraph ids: sampling was not disjoint/temporal.")
        sub, times = ns.batch, ns[TIME_ATTR]
        later = times > seed_time[sub]
        if nt == input_type:
            later[:bs] = False  # the seeds themselves (strict mode puts them 1 s after seed_time)
        if later.any():
            raise CausalityError(f"{int(later.sum())} '{nt}' node(s) later than their seed.")

    slack = 1 if strict_time else 0  # a seed's own edges carry the seed's time
    for et in batch.edge_types:
        es = batch[et]
        if "timestamp" not in es:
            continue
        sub = batch[et[2]].batch[es.edge_index[1]]
        if (es.timestamp > seed_time[sub] + slack).any():
            raise CausalityError(f"edge type {et}: edge later than the seed.")


__all__ = [
    "CausalityError", "DropAttributes", "ComposeTransforms", "has_pyg_lib", "require_pyg_lib",
    "make_seed_time", "build_neighbor_loader", "verify_batch_causality",
]

================================================================================

FILE: src\losses\__init__.py

================================================================================

"""Shared helpers for the four label-delay losses (all take LOGITS and return a scalar).

Common signature::

    loss(preds, y_obs, pnu_status, *, age_days=None, in_train_view=None)

preds         [B] (or [B, 1]) logits of the seed transactions
y_obs         [B] observed label at the label cut (0/1)
pnu_status    [B] integer code 0=P, 1=N, 2=U (``src.graph.builder.PNU_CODES``)
age_days      [B] days between the transaction and the label cut (``compute_age_days``)
in_train_view [B] bool, True before the label cut (None -> all True)

``y_true`` is never an argument: no loss can see the hidden labels. A batch with nothing to learn
from returns a zero that stays connected to the autograd graph.

The loss classes are imported lazily (module ``__getattr__``) so that each loss module can import
these helpers from the package without an import cycle.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import importlib
from typing import Any

import torch
import torch.nn as nn
from torch import Tensor

from src.data.loader import SECONDS_PER_DAY
from src.graph.builder import PNU_CODES

PNU_P: int = PNU_CODES["P"]
PNU_N: int = PNU_CODES["N"]
PNU_U: int = PNU_CODES["U"]


def prepare_inputs(
    preds: Tensor,
    y_obs: Tensor,
    pnu_status: Tensor,
    in_train_view: Tensor | None = None,
) -> tuple[Tensor, Tensor, Tensor, Tensor]:
    """Validate shapes; return ``(logits [B] float, y [B] float, pnu [B] long, view [B] bool)``."""
    if preds.dim() == 2 and preds.size(1) == 1:
        preds = preds.squeeze(1)
    if preds.dim() != 1:
        raise ValueError(f"preds must have shape [B] or [B, 1], got {tuple(preds.shape)}.")
    n = preds.numel()
    y = y_obs.reshape(-1)
    pnu = pnu_status.reshape(-1)
    if y.numel() != n or pnu.numel() != n:
        raise ValueError("preds, y_obs and pnu_status must have the same length.")
    if in_train_view is None:
        view = torch.ones(n, dtype=torch.bool, device=preds.device)
    else:
        view = in_train_view.reshape(-1).bool()
        if view.numel() != n:
            raise ValueError("in_train_view must have the same length as preds.")
    return preds, y.to(preds.dtype), pnu.long(), view


def zero_loss(preds: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    """Scalar 0 that keeps the autograd graph (an empty selection must not break ``backward``)."""
    return preds.sum() * 0.0


def compute_age_days(time: Tensor, label_cut: float) -> Tensor:
    """Age in days of each transaction at the label cut (clamped at 0)."""
    return ((float(label_cut) - time.to(torch.float64)).clamp_min(0.0) / float(SECONDS_PER_DAY)).to(torch.float32)


_LAZY: dict[str, tuple[str, str]] = {
    "NaiveBCELoss": ("src.losses.naive_bce", "NaiveBCELoss"),
    "naive_bce_loss": ("src.losses.naive_bce", "naive_bce_loss"),
    "MaturityMaskLoss": ("src.losses.maturity_mask", "MaturityMaskLoss"),
    "maturity_mask_loss": ("src.losses.maturity_mask", "maturity_mask_loss"),
    "DelayIPWLoss": ("src.losses.delay_ipw", "DelayIPWLoss"),
    "delay_ipw_loss": ("src.losses.delay_ipw", "delay_ipw_loss"),
    "lam_from_median_delay": ("src.losses.delay_ipw", "lam_from_median_delay"),
    "TimeWindowNNDFLoss": ("src.losses.tw_nndf", "TimeWindowNNDFLoss"),
    "tw_nndf_loss": ("src.losses.tw_nndf", "tw_nndf_loss"),
    "estimate_class_prior": ("src.losses.tw_nndf", "estimate_class_prior"),
    "HybridTWnnDFLoss": ("src.losses.tw_nndf", "HybridTWnnDFLoss"),
    "SlidingWindowPrior": ("src.losses.tw_nndf", "SlidingWindowPrior"),
    "MatureTemperatureTracker": ("src.losses.calibration", "MatureTemperatureTracker"),
    "trusted_negative_mask": ("src.losses.maturity_mask", "trusted_negative_mask"),
}
LOSS_CLASSES: dict[str, str] = {
    "naive_bce": "NaiveBCELoss",
    "maturity_mask": "MaturityMaskLoss",
    "delay_ipw": "DelayIPWLoss",
    "tw_nndf": "TimeWindowNNDFLoss",
}


def __getattr__(name: str) -> Any:  # lazy re-exports (PEP 562)
    if name in _LAZY:
        module, attr = _LAZY[name]
        return getattr(importlib.import_module(module), attr)
    raise AttributeError(f"module {__name__!r} has no attribute {name!r}")


def build_loss(name: str, **kwargs: Any) -> nn.Module:
    """Instantiate a loss by name, e.g. ``build_loss('delay_ipw', lam=0.23)``."""
    if name not in LOSS_CLASSES:
        raise KeyError(f"Unknown loss '{name}'. Available: {sorted(LOSS_CLASSES)}")
    return __getattr__(LOSS_CLASSES[name])(**kwargs)


__all__ = [
    "PNU_P", "PNU_N", "PNU_U", "prepare_inputs", "zero_loss", "compute_age_days", "build_loss",
    "LOSS_CLASSES", *sorted(_LAZY),
]

================================================================================

FILE: src\losses\calibration.py

================================================================================

"""Train-only calibration helpers for TW-nnDF (anti-leakage by construction).

Anti-leakage contract
---------------------
* Every function receives tensors of the TRAIN graph only (observed labels ``y_obs``, ages, P/N/U codes, logits of
  TRAIN rows). No argument can carry validation / test rows and ``y_true`` is never used.
* ``MatureTemperatureTracker`` is fed ONLY by the loss, from the mature train rows of the training step; the
  validation pass never goes through the loss, and the tracker ignores calls made without autograd.
* Temperature scaling only rescales logits; it cannot repair a biased prior by itself (KHÔNG CHẮC how much it
  helps the nnPU term: an empirical question).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass

import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch import Tensor

from src.losses import PNU_P, PNU_U

PRIOR_EPS: float = 1e-4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PriorEstimate:
    """Pooled prior plus the per-window rates / counts it was built from (logged for diagnostics)."""

    prior: float
    window_rates: tuple[float, ...]
    window_counts: tuple[int, ...]


def _view_mask(ref: Tensor, in_train_view: Tensor | None) -> Tensor:
    if in_train_view is None:
        return torch.ones_like(ref, dtype=torch.bool)
    view = in_train_view.reshape(-1).bool()
    if view.numel() != ref.numel():
        raise ValueError("in_train_view must have the same length as the other inputs.")
    return view


def _clip(p: float, eps: float) -> float:
    return float(min(max(p, eps), 1.0 - eps))


def sliding_window_prior(
    y_obs: Tensor,
    age_days: Tensor,
    window_days: float,
    *,
    in_train_view: Tensor | None = None,
    span_days: float = 14.0,
    n_windows: int = 3,
    eps: float = PRIOR_EPS,
) -> PriorEstimate:
    """Pooled positive rate of the ``n_windows`` most recent MATURE windows of ``span_days`` each.

    Window k covers ages ``[W + k*span, W + (k+1)*span)``; k = 0 is the most recent fully-reported window.
    Young rows (age < W) and rows outside the train view never enter.
    """
    if window_days <= 0 or span_days <= 0 or n_windows < 1:
        raise ValueError("window_days and span_days must be > 0 and n_windows >= 1.")
    y = y_obs.reshape(-1).float()
    age = age_days.reshape(-1).float()
    if y.numel() != age.numel():
        raise ValueError("y_obs and age_days must have the same length.")
    view = _view_mask(y, in_train_view)
    rates: list[float] = []
    counts: list[int] = []
    for k in range(n_windows):
        lo = window_days + k * span_days
        m = view & (age >= lo) & (age < lo + span_days)
        n = int(m.sum())
        if n == 0:
            continue
        rates.append(float(y[m].mean()))
        counts.append(n)
    if not counts:
        raise ValueError("No mature rows in the requested windows: cannot estimate the prior.")
    pooled = sum(r * c for r, c in zip(rates, counts)) / sum(counts)
    return PriorEstimate(_clip(pooled, eps), tuple(rates), tuple(counts))


def young_counts(
    pnu_status: Tensor, age_days: Tensor, window_days: float, in_train_view: Tensor | None = None
) -> tuple[int, int]:
    """``(n_P, n_U)`` among train-view rows younger than W (the immature region)."""
    pnu = pnu_status.reshape(-1)
    age = age_days.reshape(-1)
    if pnu.numel() != age.numel():
        raise ValueError("pnu_status and age_days must have the same length.")
    young = _view_mask(age, in_train_view) & (age < window_days)
    return int((young & (pnu == PNU_P)).sum()), int((young & (pnu == PNU_U)).sum())


def unlabeled_prior(pi_population: float, n_pos_young: int, n_unl_young: int, eps: float = PRIOR_EPS) -> float:
    """Positive prior inside the young unlabeled set: ``(pi * (n_P + n_U) - n_P) / n_U``, clipped to (0, 1)."""
    if n_unl_young <= 0:
        raise ValueError("No unlabeled young rows: cannot convert the prior.")
    hidden = pi_population * (n_pos_young + n_unl_young) - n_pos_young
    return _clip(hidden / n_unl_young, eps)

class SlidingWindowPrior:
    """pi as a function of a row's age (days before the label cut), from MATURE TRAIN rows only (observed labels).

    For a row of age ``a`` the window is the mature rows with age in ``[a + W, a + W + span)``: strictly older,
    fully reported, so only labels that were already knowable are used. ``shrink`` pulls a thin window towards the
    global mature prior (additive smoothing). Table is built once on the TRAIN graph; no val/test input exists.
    """

    def __init__(self, table: Tensor, global_prior: float, bin_days: float) -> None:
        self.table = table.detach().float().cpu()
        self.global_prior = float(global_prior)
        self.bin_days = float(bin_days)

    @classmethod
    def fit(
        cls,
        y_obs: Tensor,
        age_days: Tensor,
        window_days: float,
        in_train_view: Tensor | None = None,
        *,
        span_days: float = 14.0,
        shrink: float = 200.0,
        bin_days: float = 1.0,
        eps: float = PRIOR_EPS,
    ) -> "SlidingWindowPrior":
        if window_days <= 0 or span_days <= 0 or bin_days <= 0 or shrink < 0:
            raise ValueError("window_days, span_days, bin_days must be > 0 and shrink >= 0.")
        y = y_obs.reshape(-1).float().cpu()
        age = age_days.reshape(-1).float().cpu()
        if y.numel() != age.numel():
            raise ValueError("y_obs and age_days must have the same length.")
        view = _view_mask(y, None if in_train_view is None else in_train_view.cpu())
        mature = view & (age >= window_days)
        if int(mature.sum()) == 0:
            raise ValueError("No mature rows: cannot fit the sliding prior.")
        g = _clip(float(y[mature].mean()), eps)
        idx = (age[mature] / bin_days).floor().long()
        n_bins = int(idx.max()) + 1
        cnt = torch.bincount(idx, minlength=n_bins).double()
        pos = torch.bincount(idx, weights=y[mature].double(), minlength=n_bins)
        zero = torch.zeros(1, dtype=torch.float64)
        ccnt, cpos = torch.cat([zero, cnt.cumsum(0)]), torch.cat([zero, pos.cumsum(0)])
        off = int(math.ceil(window_days / bin_days))
        span = max(1, int(round(span_days / bin_days)))
        q = torch.arange(n_bins)
        lo = (q + off).clamp(max=n_bins)
        hi = (q + off + span).clamp(max=n_bins)
        n, k = ccnt[hi] - ccnt[lo], cpos[hi] - cpos[lo]
        num, den = k + shrink * g, n + shrink
        table = torch.where(den > 0, num / den.clamp_min(1e-12), torch.full_like(num, g))
        return cls(table.clamp(eps, 1.0 - eps).float(), g, bin_days)

    def query(self, age_days: Tensor) -> Tensor:
        """pi per age; ages beyond the table reuse the last bin (empty window -> global prior)."""
        idx = (age_days.reshape(-1).float() / self.bin_days).floor().long().clamp(0, self.table.numel() - 1)
        return self.table.to(age_days.device)[idx]

class TemperatureScaler(nn.Module):
    """One positive scalar ``T``: calibrated logit = logit / T. ``T`` is stored as ``exp(log_t)`` so it stays > 0."""

    def __init__(self, init_temperature: float = 1.0) -> None:
        super().__init__()
        if init_temperature <= 0:
            raise ValueError("init_temperature must be > 0.")
        self.log_t = nn.Parameter(torch.tensor(math.log(init_temperature)))

    @property
    def temperature(self) -> float:
        return float(self.log_t.detach().exp())

    def forward(self, logits: Tensor) -> Tensor:
        return logits / self.log_t.exp()

    def fit(self, logits: Tensor, labels: Tensor, max_iter: int = 100) -> "TemperatureScaler":
        """Minimise the BCE of ``logits / T`` against ``labels`` (0/1). Both classes are required."""
        z = logits.detach().reshape(-1).float()
        y = labels.detach().reshape(-1).float()
        if z.numel() == 0 or z.numel() != y.numel():
            raise ValueError("logits and labels must be non-empty and of equal length.")
        n_pos = int((y > 0.5).sum())
        if n_pos == 0 or n_pos == y.numel():
            raise ValueError("Both classes are required to fit the temperature.")
        opt = torch.optim.LBFGS([self.log_t], lr=0.5, max_iter=max_iter, line_search_fn="strong_wolfe")

        def closure() -> Tensor:
            opt.zero_grad()
            loss = F.binary_cross_entropy_with_logits(z / self.log_t.exp(), y)
            loss.backward()
            return loss

        opt.step(closure)
        with torch.no_grad():
            self.log_t.clamp_(-4.0, 4.0)
        return self

    def fit_on_mature(
        self,
        logits: Tensor,
        labels: Tensor,
        age_days: Tensor,
        window_days: float,
        *,
        in_train_view: Tensor | None = None,
        max_iter: int = 100,
    ) -> "TemperatureScaler":
        """Fit on the MATURE train rows only (``age >= W``); every other row is ignored by construction."""
        age = age_days.reshape(-1)
        mask = _view_mask(age, in_train_view) & (age >= window_days)
        return self.fit(logits.reshape(-1)[mask], labels.reshape(-1)[mask], max_iter)


TemperatureScaling = TemperatureScaler  # name used in the project brief


class MatureTemperatureTracker:
    """Fits ``T`` on the maturity-masked subset of the TRAIN set (labels ``y_obs``), once per epoch.

    The loss calls ``collect`` with the detached logits and 0/1 labels of the mature rows (P, N and trusted-U) of
    each TRAINING step; ``refit`` (epoch end) fits ``T`` on what was collected and clears the buffer. Epoch 1 uses
    ``T = init_temperature``. Logits come from the training pass (dropout on), so ``T`` is slightly stale and
    noisy (limitation, KHÔNG CHẮC). Validation data cannot reach this object: nothing but the loss feeds it.
    The tracker is a plain object (not an ``nn.Module``), so it stays on the CPU and is never in an optimizer.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        max_samples: int = 200_000,
        t_min: float = 0.25,
        t_max: float = 4.0,
        init_temperature: float = 1.0,
    ) -> None:
        if max_samples < 2 or not 0 < t_min <= init_temperature <= t_max:
            raise ValueError("need max_samples >= 2 and 0 < t_min <= init_temperature <= t_max.")
        self.max_samples = int(max_samples)
        self.t_min, self.t_max = float(t_min), float(t_max)
        self.scaler = TemperatureScaler(init_temperature)
        self._z: list[Tensor] = []
        self._y: list[Tensor] = []
        self._n = 0

    @property
    def temperature(self) -> float:
        return self.scaler.temperature

    @property
    def n_collected(self) -> int:
        return self._n

    def collect(self, logits: Tensor, labels: Tensor) -> None:
        z = logits.detach().reshape(-1).float()
        y = labels.detach().reshape(-1).float()
        if z.numel() != y.numel():
            raise ValueError("logits and labels must have the same length.")
        room = self.max_samples - self._n
        if room <= 0 or z.numel() == 0:
            return
        z, y = z[:room], y[:room]  # train batches are shuffled, so the first rows are a random sample
        self._z.append(z.cpu())
        self._y.append(y.cpu())
        self._n += int(z.numel())

    def collected(self) -> tuple[Tensor, Tensor]:
        if not self._z:
            return torch.empty(0), torch.empty(0)
        return torch.cat(self._z), torch.cat(self._y)

    def refit(self) -> float:
        """Fit ``T`` on the collected rows (needs both classes, else keep the old ``T``); clear the buffer."""
        z, y = self.collected()
        self._z, self._y, self._n = [], [], 0
        n_pos = int((y > 0.5).sum())
        if z.numel() == 0 or n_pos == 0 or n_pos == z.numel():
            return self.temperature
        self.scaler.fit(z, y)
        with torch.no_grad():
            self.scaler.log_t.clamp_(math.log(self.t_min), math.log(self.t_max))
        return self.temperature


@torch.no_grad()
def calibrated_prior(logits_unlabeled: Tensor, scaler: TemperatureScaler, eps: float = PRIOR_EPS) -> float:
    """Mean calibrated probability over unlabeled rows (model-based prior estimate; not wired into the trainer)."""
    p = torch.sigmoid(scaler(logits_unlabeled.reshape(-1).float()))
    return _clip(float(p.mean()), eps)


__all__ = [
    "PriorEstimate", "sliding_window_prior", "young_counts", "unlabeled_prior", "TemperatureScaler",
    "TemperatureScaling", "MatureTemperatureTracker", "SlidingWindowPrior", "calibrated_prior", "PRIOR_EPS",
]

================================================================================

FILE: src\losses\canary.py

================================================================================

"""Canary hooks for the loss layer and the model (reusable by tests and, in debug mode, by the trainer).

Invariants checked
  * naive_bce     : every U row has a NON-zero gradient (U is read as negative).
  * maturity_mask : every U row has exactly ZERO gradient and the loss value does not change when U
                    logits change.
  * tw_nndf       : U rows have zero gradient ONLY while the negative-risk clamp is active
                    (``neg_risk_raw < 0``); outside that regime the U gradient is non-zero by design.
  * model         : logits do not depend on label fields (y_obs, y_true, pnu) of the batch.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import copy
from typing import Callable, Iterable

import torch
from torch import Tensor

LossFn = Callable[..., Tensor]


def logits_grad(loss_fn: LossFn, preds: Tensor, y_obs: Tensor, pnu_status: Tensor, **kwargs) -> tuple[Tensor, Tensor]:
    """Return ``(loss value, d loss / d logits)``; the gradient is all zeros if the loss is detached."""
    p = preds.detach().clone().float().requires_grad_(True)
    loss = loss_fn(p, y_obs, pnu_status, **kwargs)
    if not loss.requires_grad:
        return loss.detach(), torch.zeros_like(p)
    (grad,) = torch.autograd.grad(loss, p, allow_unused=True)
    return loss.detach(), torch.zeros_like(p) if grad is None else grad


def assert_zero_grad_on_status(grad: Tensor, pnu_status: Tensor, status: int, name: str = "loss") -> None:
    rows = pnu_status.reshape(-1) == status
    if not bool(rows.any()):
        raise AssertionError(f"{name}: vacuous check, no row with status {status}.")
    if bool((grad[rows] != 0).any()):
        raise AssertionError(f"{name}: rows with status {status} received a non-zero gradient.")


def assert_nonzero_grad_on_status(
    grad: Tensor, pnu_status: Tensor, status: int, name: str = "loss", require_all: bool = True
) -> None:
    rows = pnu_status.reshape(-1) == status
    if not bool(rows.any()):
        raise AssertionError(f"{name}: vacuous check, no row with status {status}.")
    nz = grad[rows] != 0
    ok = bool(nz.all()) if require_all else bool(nz.any())
    if not ok:
        raise AssertionError(f"{name}: rows with status {status} have a zero gradient where non-zero is expected.")


def assert_loss_invariant_to_status(
    loss_fn: LossFn,
    preds: Tensor,
    y_obs: Tensor,
    pnu_status: Tensor,
    status: int,
    *,
    scale: float = 5.0,
    seed: int = 0,
    **kwargs,
) -> None:
    """The loss value must be bit-identical after perturbing the logits of every row with ``status``."""
    rows = pnu_status.reshape(-1) == status
    if not bool(rows.any()):
        raise AssertionError(f"vacuous check, no row with status {status}.")
    g = torch.Generator().manual_seed(seed)
    noise = torch.randn(int(rows.sum()), generator=g).to(preds.device, preds.dtype) * scale
    other = preds.detach().clone()
    other[rows] = other[rows] + noise
    base = loss_fn(preds.detach(), y_obs, pnu_status, **kwargs)
    changed = loss_fn(other, y_obs, pnu_status, **kwargs)
    if not torch.equal(base, changed):
        raise AssertionError(f"loss changed ({float(base)!r} -> {float(changed)!r}) when status-{status} logits changed.")


def model_leakage_hook(
    model: torch.nn.Module,
    batch,
    label_fields: Iterable[str] = ("y_obs", "y_true", "pnu"),
    node_type: str = "transaction",
    seed: int = 0,
) -> None:
    """Scramble the label fields of the batch and require identical logits (eval mode, no dropout).

    Catches any path through which a label could reach the forward pass (e.g. a future label feature).
    """
    was_training = model.training
    model.eval()
    try:
        with torch.no_grad():
            base = model(batch)
            scrambled = copy.deepcopy(batch)
            store = scrambled[node_type]
            g = torch.Generator().manual_seed(seed)
            changed = False
            for f in label_fields:
                if f in store:
                    v = store[f]
                    perm = torch.randperm(v.numel(), generator=g).to(v.device)
                    new = v[perm]
                    changed = changed or not torch.equal(new, v)
                    store[f] = new
            if not changed:
                raise AssertionError("vacuous hook: no label field found, or scrambling changed nothing.")
            other = model(scrambled)
    finally:
        model.train(was_training)
    if not torch.equal(base, other):
        raise AssertionError("model output depends on label fields: a label reaches the forward pass.")
